Question 85

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  THERMOSETTING (Bakelite): permanently cross-linked. Cannot be remelted. Cannot be recycled by melting.



Question 86

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  NH4NO3 thermal decomposition above 200C is exothermic and releases gas rapidly. Can be explosive if: contaminated, large quantity, shock/fire applied.



Question 87

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  HABER PROCESS: N2(from air)+H2(from steam reforming of CH4)=NH3.



Question 88

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Ostwald: starts with AMMONIA (Haber). 4NH3+5O2=(Pt/Rh, 850C)=4NO+6H2O. Then NO=NO2=HNO3.



Question 89

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  PETROLEUM: from ancient MARINE ORGANISMS (mainly phytoplankton) buried under sediment, heat and pressure over millions of years.



Question 90

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Fractionating column: temperature decreases bottom to top. HEAVY molecules (bitumen) condense at BOTTOM.



Question 91

✅ ANSWER: D

📝 EXPLANATION:  OIL OF VITRIOL=conc H2SO4. Most important industrial chemical.



Question 92

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Cement setting=HYDRATION: calcium silicates react with water. Interlocking crystals form. NOT just drying.



Question 93

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  NPK label: first=%N, second=%P as P2O5, third=%K as K2O. 20-10-10: 20%N, 10%P2O5, 10%K2O.



Question 94

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Al recycling: uses only ~5% of energy of primary production. Very efficient. Al recycled infinitely.



Question 95

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  BIODIESEL: vegetable oils+methanol=(NaOH catalyst)=FAME (fatty acid methyl esters)+glycerol.



Question 96

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  CHLOR-ALKALI: brine electrolysis. Anode: Cl2. Cathode: H2+OH-. Products: Cl2, H2, NaOH.



Question 97

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  OSTWALD PROCESS: NH3=(Pt/Rh, 850C)=NO=NO2=HNO3. Converts ammonia to nitric acid.



Question 98

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  3Rs: REDUCE (don't generate waste, BEST) = REUSE (extend life) = RECYCLE (recover material). Landfill = worst.



Question 99

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  POLYESTER (Terylene/PET): DIOL+DICARBOXYLIC ACID. e.g. ethylene glycol+terephthalic acid+H2O.



Question 100

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  NATURAL RUBBER=polyisoprene. Monomer=ISOPRENE.



Question 101

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  ETHENE: most versatile industrial organic. Made by cracking. Used for polyethylene, PVC, ethanol, styrene, etc.



Question 102

✅ ANSWER: A

📝 EXPLANATION:  ANFO=Ammonium Nitrate+Fuel Oil. Common commercial explosive. NH4NO3 provides oxidiser, fuel oil provides fuel.



Question 103

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  H2SO4 (SULPHURIC ACID): most produced industrial chemical. ~250 million tonnes/year. Used in almost every industry.



Question 104

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Industrial O3: CORONA DISCHARGE - high voltage discharge through O2 or dry air.



Question 105

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  ZIEGLER-NATTA (TiCl4+AlR3): makes STEREOREGULAR polymers. Better mechanical properties.



Question 106

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  CEMENT: limestone(CaCO3)+clay(SiO2,Al2O3)=heated in rotary kiln=calcium silicates+aluminates.



Question 107

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  PHOSPHATE fertilisers (superphosphate, triple superphosphate) provide PHOSPHORUS. Essential for root development, flowering, DNA, ATP.



Question 108

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Cl2 dissolves in water: Cl2 + H2O → HCl + HOCl. HCl turns litmus red; HOCl (hypochlorous acid) bleaches/decolorises it.



Question 109

✅ ANSWER: D

📝 EXPLANATION:  Cl2 is a yellow-green gas at room temperature. Br2 is a red-brown liquid; I2 is a dark grey solid; F2 is pale yellow gas.



Question 110

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  SO2 decolorises acidified KMnO4 (reducing agent test). It also turns K2Cr2O7 paper from orange to green.



Question 111

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Haber process: N2 + 3H2 ⇌ 2NH3. Hydrogen reacts with nitrogen over Fe catalyst to make ammonia.



Question 112

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Industrial O2 is obtained by fractional distillation of liquefied air (b.p. O2 = −183°C, N2 = −196°C).



Question 113

✅ ANSWER: A

📝 EXPLANATION:  Sulphur has two common allotropes: rhombic (α-S, stable <96°C) and monoclinic (β-S, stable 96–119°C).



Question 114

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Conc. H2SO4 has extremely strong affinity for water — it dehydrates sugar (C12H22O11 → 12C + 11H2O) and desiccates compounds.



Question 115

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Contact Process: S → SO2 → SO3 (V2O5 catalyst) → H2SO4 (via oleum). It manufactures sulfuric acid.



Question 116

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  CO2 itself is the product of complete combustion of carbon. The C is already at +4 oxidation state — cannot oxidise further.



Question 117

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  White P4 is highly reactive, glows in dark (slow oxidation), toxic, and stored in water. Red P is less reactive and safer.



Question 118

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Hydrogen burns with a characteristic squeaky pop when a burning splint is applied to a tube of H2.



Question 119

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  N2 is inert — used in packaging perishables, purging containers, and in incandescent light bulbs to prevent filament oxidation.



Question 120

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Fluorine: smallest atom, highest electronegativity (4.0 on Pauling scale) — strongest pull on bonding electrons.



Question 121

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  In the blast furnace: CO reduces iron ore → CO + Fe2O3 → Fe + CO2. Carbon monoxide is the main reducing agent.



Question 122

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Oxygen relights a glowing splint — the standard lab test for O2.



Question 123

✅ ANSWER: A

📝 EXPLANATION:  Carbon allotropes include diamond, graphite, amorphous carbon, and buckminsterfullerene (C60 buckyball).



Question 124

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Cl2 + 2NaOH → NaCl + NaOCl + H2O (cold, dilute). Products are sodium chloride and sodium hypochlorite (bleach).



Question 125

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Ostwald Process: NH3 oxidised over Pt to NO, then NO + O2 → NO2, then NO2 + H2O → HNO3.



Question 126

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Nitrogen fixation: conversion of N2 gas to ammonia (NH3) or other nitrogen compounds. Industrial: Haber process; Biological: Rhizobium bacteria.



Question 127

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  SO3 + H2O → H2SO4. Sulphur trioxide reacts vigorously with water to form sulphuric acid.



Question 128

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  H3PO4 has three ionisable hydrogen atoms — it is triprotic (tribasic), though the third dissociation is very weak.



Question 129

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  F2, Cl2, and Br2 are all more reactive than I2 and can displace I⁻: Cl2 + 2KI → 2KCl + I2.



Question 130

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  SiO2 (silica) is the main component of glass. Pure SiO2 is used in optical fibres for communications.



Question 131

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Ozone (O3) is an allotrope of oxygen (O2). O3 is more reactive, has a pungent smell, and absorbs UV in the stratosphere.



Question 132

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  NaCl is mined as rock salt or obtained by evaporation of sea/brine, then purified by recrystallisation.



Question 133

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  The Frasch process: superheated water and compressed air are pumped underground to melt and bring sulphur to the surface.



Question 134

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Iodine (I2) forms a blue-black complex with starch. This is the standard test for starch (or iodine).



Question 135

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  SO3 is an acidic oxide — it reacts with water to form H2SO4. CaO, Na2O, and MgO are basic oxides.



Question 136

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  NO2 is a reddish-brown gas with a pungent smell. It is responsible for the brown colour of smog.



Question 137

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Steam reforming: CH4 + H2O → CO + 3H2 (at ~800°C with Ni catalyst). This is the main industrial source of H2 for Haber.



Question 138

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Lab preparation: MnO2 + 4HCl(conc) → MnCl2 + Cl2↑ + 2H2O. MnO2 oxidises Cl⁻ to Cl2.



Question 139

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Diamond has no free electrons (all 4 carbon electrons are in covalent bonds) — it is an excellent electrical insulator.



Question 140

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  In graphite, each carbon uses 3 bonds in layers; the 4th electron is delocalised within the layer, allowing electrical conduction.



Question 141

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  SO2 is used as a food preservative (E220) and bleaching agent for wood pulp and fabrics due to its reducing/antioxidant properties.



Question 142

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  S + O2 → SO2 (combustion). SO2 + ½O2 → SO3 only with V2O5 catalyst (Contact process step 2).



Question 143

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Halogen reactivity decreases down Group 7: F2 > Cl2 > Br2 > I2 (smaller atoms attract electrons more strongly).



Question 144

✅ ANSWER: A

📝 EXPLANATION:  NH3 is a pungent gas that turns moist red litmus blue (it is alkaline: NH3 + H2O → NH4⁺ + OH⁻).



Question 145

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  The blast furnace reduces iron ore to pig iron (~4% C, brittle). Steel and wrought iron require further processing.



Question 146

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Al reacts with O2 to form a thin, dense Al2O3 layer that prevents further oxidation — self-passivation.



Question 147

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Iron ore is primarily haematite (Fe2O3) and magnetite (Fe3O4). These are reduced in the blast furnace.



Question 148

✅ ANSWER: A

📝 EXPLANATION:  Na is too reactive (high in reactivity series) to be obtained by carbon reduction. It requires electrolysis of molten NaCl.



Question 149

✅ ANSWER: A

📝 EXPLANATION:  Thermite: 2Al + Fe2O3 → Al2O3 + 2Fe. Al is more reactive than Fe — the highly exothermic reaction produces molten iron.



Question 150

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Zinc (Zn) is extracted by roasting ZnS to ZnO then reducing with carbon/coke: ZnO + C → Zn + CO. Na, Ca, Al require electrolysis.



Question 151

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  The 3d and 4s subshells are close in energy in transition metals, allowing different numbers of electrons to be involved in bonding.



Question 152

✅ ANSWER: A

📝 EXPLANATION:  Copper ores include malachite (Cu2CO3(OH)2) and chalcopyrite (CuFeS2, most abundant). Cu is extracted by smelting and electrolytic refining.



Question 153

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Steel hardness increases with carbon content. High-carbon steel is hard but brittle; low-carbon (mild) steel is soft and ductile.



Question 154

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Stainless steel: ~18% Cr (+ ~8% Ni). Cr forms Cr2O3 layer, giving corrosion resistance.



Question 155

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  The reactivity series ranks metals by their tendency to lose electrons (be oxidised). More reactive metals displace less reactive ones.



Question 156

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Zn (above Fe in reactivity series) is preferentially oxidised, protecting the iron even if the coating is scratched.



Question 157

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Galena (PbS) is the main ore of lead. It is roasted to PbO then reduced with carbon.



Question 158

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Mercury (Hg) is the only metal that is liquid at room temperature (m.p. = −39°C).



Question 159

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Electrolytic refining of copper: crude Cu (impure) anode, pure Cu cathode, in CuSO4/H2SO4 electrolyte.



Question 160

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Pig iron (~4% C) is converted to steel by blowing oxygen through it (Basic Oxygen Furnace) to remove excess C, then controlled addition of alloying metals.



Question 161

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Bauxite (hydrated aluminium oxide) is the main ore of aluminium. Cryolite is the solvent used in electrolysis.



Question 162

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Gold is unreactive — it does not react with water, dilute acids, or most chemicals. It is a noble metal.



Question 163

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Tin is non-toxic and food-safe. However, if the tin coating is scratched, iron corrodes faster (Sn is less reactive than Fe).



Question 164

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Iron (Fe) with promoters Al2O3 and K2O is the catalyst for N2 + 3H2 ⇌ 2NH3 in the Haber process.



Question 165

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Fe2O3 is iron(III) oxide. Fe is in the +3 oxidation state (2Fe + 3O = 0: Fe = +3). IUPAC names transition metal compounds by oxidation state.



Question 166

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Alloys combine two or more metals (or metal + non-metal) to obtain improved physical and chemical properties.



Question 167

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Limestone (CaCO3) decomposes to CaO, which reacts with silica (SiO2) impurity to form slag (CaSiO3) that floats on molten iron.



Question 168

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  CO is produced: C + CO2 → 2CO, then reduces iron ore: Fe2O3 + 3CO → 2Fe + 3CO2. CO is the principal reducing agent.



Question 169

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Sodium burns in excess O2 to form sodium peroxide: 2Na + O2 → Na2O2. In limited O2: 4Na + O2 → 2Na2O.



Question 170

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Zone refining produces ultra-pure Si and Ge for semiconductor use — impurities concentrate in a molten zone that moves along the rod.



Question 171

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  K is below Na in Group 1 — larger atom, lower IE, more reactive. K+H2O produces enough heat to ignite H2.



Question 172

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Roasting (heating in air): 2ZnS + 3O2 → 2ZnO + 2SO2. The sulphide is converted to oxide, releasing SO2.



Question 173

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Zn is above Cu in reactivity series: Zn + CuSO4 → ZnSO4 + Cu. Zn cannot displace Na, K, or Mg.



Question 174

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Titanium is lightweight, very strong, and highly corrosion-resistant — used in aircraft, medical implants, and chemical plants.



Question 175

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Sodium is extracted from rock salt (NaCl) by the Downs process (electrolysis of molten NaCl).



Question 176

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Wrought iron: <0.2% C, malleable, tough. Cast/pig iron: ~4% C, brittle, hard. Steel: 0.2–1.5% C.



Question 177

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Ca + 2H2O → Ca(OH)2 + H2↑. Calcium reacts steadily with cold water, forming calcium hydroxide (slightly soluble) and hydrogen.



Question 178

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Hot air (or oxygen-enriched air) blast ignites the coke to produce CO2, which then reacts with more coke to form CO (the reducing agent).



Question 179

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  An amalgam is an alloy of mercury with one or more other metals (e.g. dental amalgam = Hg + Ag + Sn + Cu).



Question 180

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Metallic bonding: positive metal cations are held together by a sea of delocalised (free) electrons throughout the structure.



Question 181

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Bronze = copper + tin (Cu + Sn). Brass = copper + zinc (Cu + Zn).



Question 182

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  C6H12O6 → 2C2H5OH + 2CO2. Yeast contains enzymes (zymase) that convert glucose to ethanol and CO2 anaerobically.



Question 183

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Carboxylic acids contain the −COOH (carboxyl) group. e.g. CH3COOH (ethanoic/acetic acid).



Question 184

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Alkenes contain C=C double bonds that react by addition: Br2 adds across C=C, HBr adds, H2O adds, etc.



Question 185

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Saponification: RCOO-glycerol + NaOH → RCOONa (soap) + glycerol. Alkaline hydrolysis of fats/oils.



Question 186

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  CH3CH2OH has 2 carbons with −OH group on C1 → ethanol (IUPAC: ethanol or ethan-1-ol).



Question 187

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  CH3CHO (ethanal) contains −CHO group (aldehyde). CH3COCH3 is a ketone; CH3COOH is an acid; CH3OH is an alcohol.



Question 188

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Esterification: R-OH + R'COOH ⇌ R'COOR + H2O (with H2SO4 catalyst). Produces ester + water.



Question 189

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Polymerisation: n(CH2=CH2) → (−CH2−CH2−)n. Many monomer units join to form a polymer.



Question 190

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Alkanes (saturated hydrocarbons): CnH2n+2. e.g. methane CH4 (n=1): 2(1)+2 = 4 H atoms.



Question 191

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Cracking breaks long-chain alkanes from crude oil into shorter, more useful alkenes (for polymers) and alkanes (for fuels).



Question 192

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Fractional distillation of crude oil separates components by boiling point into fractions (gases, petrol, kerosene, diesel, lubricants, bitumen).



Question 193

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Starch and cellulose are natural polymers of glucose. Nylon, PVC, and polythene are synthetic polymers.



Question 194

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Markovnikov: in addition of HX to alkene, H goes to carbon already bearing more H atoms; X goes to more substituted carbon.



Question 195

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Alkynes (contain C≡C triple bond): CnH2n−2. e.g. ethyne C2H2: 2(2)−2 = 2 H atoms ✓.



Question 196

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Nylon (polyamide) is a condensation polymer — formed by repeated condensation (elimination of H2O or HCl) between monomers.



Question 197

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Ethanol oxidation: C2H5OH → CH3CHO (ethanal) with mild oxidant; → CH3COOH (ethanoic acid) with excess oxidant.



Question 198

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Amines contain −NH2 (amino) group. e.g. methylamine CH3NH2.



Question 199

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Alcohol dehydration with conc. H2SO4 at 180°C: CH3CH2OH → CH2=CH2 + H2O. An alkene is formed.



Question 200

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Ethanoic acid (acetic acid): CH3COOH. It is a 2-carbon carboxylic acid.



Question 201

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Benzene (C6H6) is the simplest aromatic compound with a ring of 6 carbons and delocalised π electrons.



Question 202

✅ ANSWER: A

📝 EXPLANATION:  Tollens' reagent: RCHO + Ag(NH3)2⁺ → Ag mirror + RCOO⁻. Aldehydes are oxidised (silver mirror forms); ketones do not react.



Question 203

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Ethane: C2H6. The prefix 'eth-' denotes 2 carbon atoms. Meth(1), Eth(2), Prop(3), But(4), Pent(5).



Question 204

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Ethane (C2H6) is a saturated alkane — all C-C single bonds. Ethene and ethyne have double/triple bonds; benzene is aromatic.



Question 205

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Concentrated H2SO4 catalyses esterification by protonating the carbonyl oxygen, activating the acid for nucleophilic attack.



Question 206

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  CH3COCH3: 3 carbons, ketone (C=O not at end) on C2 → propan-2-one. Common name: acetone.



Question 207

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Addition polymerisation requires monomers with C=C double bonds (e.g. ethene, propene, chloroethene/vinyl chloride).



Question 208

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Alcohols react with Na metal: the OH hydrogen is replaced, releasing H2 gas and forming sodium alkoxide.



Question 209

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Proteins are condensation polymers of amino acids linked by peptide bonds (−CO−NH−).



Question 210

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Vulcanisation: heating natural rubber with S creates S-S cross-links between polymer chains, making rubber harder, stronger, and more elastic.



Question 211

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Primary alcohol: −OH is on a carbon attached to only one other carbon. CH3CH2OH (ethanol) is primary. (CH3)2CHOH is secondary; (CH3)3COH is tertiary.



Question 212

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Industrial ethanol: C2H4 + H2O → C2H5OH (steam hydration of ethene at 300°C, 70 atm, H3PO4/silica catalyst).



Question 213

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Fats and oils are triglycerides — esters of glycerol (propan-1,2,3-triol) and three fatty acid molecules.



Question 214

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Alkanes undergo free radical substitution with halogens in UV light: CH4 + Cl2 → CH3Cl + HCl (via Cl• radical chain mechanism).



Question 215

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  The −COOH group: the C=O withdraws electrons, making O-H more polar and facilitating H⁺ donation. Carboxylic acids are weak acids.



Question 216

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Industrially, acetic acid is produced by methanol carbonylation (Monsanto process: CH3OH + CO → CH3COOH) or by ethanol oxidation.



Question 217

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Unsaturated compounds (alkenes, alkynes, aromatics) decolorise bromine water (Br2/H2O) by addition across C=C or C≡C bonds.



Question 218

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Biodiesel: triglycerides (vegetable oil) + methanol (with NaOH catalyst) → fatty acid methyl esters (FAME) + glycerol. This is transesterification.



Question 219

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Haber process feeds: N2 (from fractional distillation of air) + 3H2 (from steam reforming of CH4) → 2NH3.



Question 220

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  ~80% of ammonia produced goes to fertiliser manufacturing (ammonium nitrate, ammonium phosphate, urea) to increase crop yields.



Question 221

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Step 1: S → SO2; Step 2: 2SO2 + O2 → 2SO3 (V2O5, 450°C, 1–2 atm); Step 3: SO3 + H2SO4 → H2S2O7 (oleum) → H2SO4.



Question 222

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Ostwald: 4NH3 + 5O2 → 4NO + 6H2O (over Pt at 900°C); 2NO + O2 → 2NO2; 3NO2 + H2O → 2HNO3 + NO.



Question 223

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Petroleum is a complex mixture of hydrocarbons formed from ancient marine organisms over millions of years.



Question 224

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Downs process: electrolysis of molten NaCl at ~580°C → Na (cathode) + Cl2 (anode). Steel cathode and graphite anode.



Question 225

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Synthetic detergents (sulfonates, etc.) form soluble salts with Ca²⁺/Mg²⁺ — they work in hard water unlike soap which forms scum.



Question 226

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Chlor-alkali process: electrolysis of brine (NaCl aq.) → Cl2 (anode), H2 (cathode), NaOH (formed in solution).



Question 227

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  PVC polymer: n(CH2=CHCl) → (−CH2−CHCl−)n. The monomer is chloroethene (vinyl chloride, CH2=CHCl).



Question 228

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Limestone uses: cement (CaCO3 + clay → heated), glass (SiO2 + CaCO3 + Na2CO3), blast furnace (slag formation), neutralising acid soils.



Question 229

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Neoprene: poly(chloroprene), polymerisation of CH2=CCl-CH=CH2 (2-chlorobutadiene). More resistant to oil and heat than natural rubber.



Question 230

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Petrol (PMS) and diesel (AGO) are the largest petroleum refinery products by volume, used in vehicles.



Question 231

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Haber optimum conditions: 150–300 atm, ~450°C, Fe catalyst. High P favours more NH3 (fewer moles on product side).



Question 232

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  2NH3 + CO2 → NH2COONH4 → (NH2)2CO + H2O. Urea is produced from ammonia and CO2 in the industrial process.



Question 233

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Nitrogen-based fertilisers from Haber process cause: N2O (greenhouse gas), NO (acid rain precursor), and nitrate runoff → eutrophication.



Question 234

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Biodegradable plastics (e.g. polylactic acid) decompose by microorganisms, reducing persistent plastic pollution in land and water.



Question 235

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Catalytic reforming (with Pt catalyst) converts low-octane straight-chain hydrocarbons to high-octane branched and aromatic compounds for better fuel.



Question 236

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Refinery gases (LPG: propane, butane) have the lowest boiling points (<40°C) and exit at the top of the fractionating column.



Question 237

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Soap manufacture: fat/oil + NaOH (hot) → soap (sodium salt of fatty acid) + glycerol. This is saponification.



Question 238

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Cracking: large alkane molecules → smaller alkanes + alkenes (e.g. C16H34 → C8H18 + C8H16). Produces fuel and monomers.



Question 239

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Bakelite is a thermosetting polymer — it permanently hardens when moulded (cross-linked network). Cannot be resoftened by heat.



Question 240

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Ethene (for polyethylene) and propene (for polypropylene) from petroleum cracking are the most important monomers for plastics.



Question 241

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Nigeria's agriculture relies on nitrogenous fertilisers (urea, ammonium sulphate) produced from Haber process ammonia to improve crop yields.



Question 242

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Portland cement: limestone (CaCO3) + clay (SiO2, Al2O3, Fe2O3) heated in a rotary kiln at ~1450°C → clinker → cement.



Question 243

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  H2SO4 + NH3 → (NH4)2SO4 (ammonium sulphate fertiliser); H2SO4 + Ca3(PO4)2 → superphosphate fertiliser.



Question 244

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Synthetic fibres (nylon, polyester, acrylic) have superior tensile strength, durability, water resistance, and lower cost than natural fibres.



Question 245

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Bioethanol (from fermentation) is blended with petrol to make gasohol (e.g. E10 = 10% ethanol), reducing fossil fuel dependence.



Question 246

✅ ANSWER: D

📝 EXPLANATION:  Chlor-alkali products: NaOH, Cl2, H2 (from brine electrolysis). Na2SO4 is not a product of chlor-alkali.



Question 247

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Coal gasification: C + H2O → CO + H2 (water gas/syngas). Syngas is used as fuel and for making methanol, ammonia, etc.



Question 248

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Soda-lime glass: Na2CO3 + CaCO3 + SiO2 → Na2CaSi3O9 + CO2. Heated to ~1500°C, molten glass forms on cooling.



Question 249

✅ ANSWER: D

📝 EXPLANATION:  Bitumen is the heaviest, highest-boiling fraction of crude oil — used in road surfacing and waterproofing.



Question 250

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Petrochemicals are chemicals derived from crude oil and natural gas through refining and cracking (e.g. ethylene, benzene, methanol).



Question 251

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Promoters (K2O, Al2O3) are added to the Fe catalyst to increase its activity (K2O) and prevent sintering/increase surface area (Al2O3).



Question 252

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Polystyrene: n(C6H5CH=CH2) → (−C6H5CH−CH2−)n. Used in packaging, insulation, and disposable cups.



Question 253

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  N% by mass: urea (NH2)2CO = 28/60 = 46.7%; NH4NO3 = 28/80 = 35%; (NH4)2SO4 = 28/132 = 21.2%. Urea has highest N%.



Question 254

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  The Claus process recovers sulphur from H2S gas in natural gas and petroleum refining: H2S + SO2 → S + H2O.



Question 255

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Cl2 uses: PVC manufacture (most), water disinfection, bleaching powder/bleach (NaOCl), HCl manufacture, pharmaceuticals.





🎯  Mixed JAMB Practice Questions (All Topics)





Mixed JAMB Past Questions — All Topics

HOW TO USE: These questions span all JAMB Chemistry topics from 1983-2025.

Attempt each question before checking the answer. Time yourself: aim for 40 questions in 30 minutes.

💡 EXAM TIP:  EXAM TIPS: (1) Watch for NOT/EXCEPT/INCORRECT. (2) Eliminate wrong options first. (3) For calculations: formula -> substitute -> check units. (4) Never leave blank.

🧠 MNEMONIC:  QUICK FORMULAS: Moles=Mass/MM | pH+pOH=14 | P1V1=P2V2 | V1/T1=V2/T2 | C1V1=C2V2 | Q=It | mass=(MIt)/(nF)
