Q309.  (JAMB 2011)  

The process where soluble starch is converted to glucose by enzymes is called:

A.  Fermentation

B.  Saponification

C.  Hydrolysis

D.  Oxidation







  📋  ANSWERS & DETAILED EXPLANATIONS  



Question 1

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Sodium is oxidized from 0 to +1 (Na → Na⁺), so it is the reducing agent. Water is reduced (H from +1 to 0 in H₂).



Question 2

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Dissolving sugar in water is a physical change — no new substance is formed. Sugar can be recovered by evaporation. The other options involve chemical reactions.



Question 3

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  CH₃CH₂OH has two carbon atoms with a hydroxyl (-OH) group. IUPAC name: eth- (2 carbons) + -anol (alcohol) = ethanol.



Question 4

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Molar mass of NaOH = 40g/mol. Moles = 4/40 = 0.1 mol. Volume = 500cm³ = 0.5dm³. Concentration = 0.1/0.5 = 0.2 mol/dm³.



Question 5

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Using C₁V₁ = C₂V₂: 0.1 × 25 = C₂ × 12.5. C₂ = 2.5/12.5 = 0.2M.



Question 6

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  In KMnO₄: K = +1, each O = -2. So +1 + Mn + 4(-2) = 0. Mn - 7 = -1. Mn = +7.



Question 7

✅ ANSWER: D

📝 EXPLANATION:  Noble gases have the highest ionization energies due to their complete outer shells. Ne has the highest first IE among these options.



Question 8

✅ ANSWER: A

📝 EXPLANATION:  Using Charles' law: V₁/T₁ = V₂/T₂. T₁ = 300K, T₂ = 400K. V₂ = 2.5 × 400/300 = 3.33L.



Question 9

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Carboxylic acids contain the -COOH (carboxyl) group. Examples: CH₃COOH (ethanoic acid), HCOOH (methanoic acid).



Question 10

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Acid + carbonate → salt + water + carbon dioxide. E.g., 2HCl + Na₂CO₃ → 2NaCl + H₂O + CO₂.



Question 11

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Potassium (atomic number 19) has electronic configuration 2,8,8,1, meaning it has 4 electron shells, placing it in Period 4.



Question 12

✅ ANSWER: A

📝 EXPLANATION:  Mole ratios: C = 40/12 = 3.33; H = 6.7/1 = 6.7; O = 53.3/16 = 3.33. Divide by 3.33: C:H:O = 1:2:1. Empirical formula = CH₂O.



Question 13

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Evaporation occurs at the surface of a liquid at any temperature below boiling point. High-energy surface molecules escape into the vapour phase.



Question 14

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Isomers have the same molecular formula (same number and types of atoms) but different structural arrangements, giving them different chemical and physical properties.



Question 15

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  The Haber process uses finely divided iron (with promoters like K₂O and Al₂O₃) as a catalyst for N₂ + 3H₂ ⇌ 2NH₃.



Question 16

✅ ANSWER: D

📝 EXPLANATION:  Acid strength increases down Group 17 (halogens). HI is the strongest hydrohalic acid because the H-I bond is weakest (longest, easiest to break), giving most complete ionization.



Question 17

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Recrystallization purifies solids by exploiting the difference in solubility at different temperatures. Impurities remain in solution while the pure compound crystallizes out on cooling.



Question 18

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Na transfers one electron to Cl, forming Na⁺ and Cl⁻ ions held together by electrostatic attraction. This is an ionic (electrovalent) bond.



Question 19

✅ ANSWER: A

📝 EXPLANATION:  Moles = mass / molar mass = 32/32 = 1 mole. Since sulfur is monatomic in this context, there is 1 mole of atoms.



Question 20

✅ ANSWER: A

📝 EXPLANATION:  Allotropes are different physical forms of the same element. Diamond and graphite are both pure carbon but with different crystal structures and therefore different properties.



Question 21

✅ ANSWER: A

📝 EXPLANATION:  Ethene undergoes addition reaction with Br₂: CH₂=CH₂ + Br₂ → CH₂BrCH₂Br (1,2-dibromoethane). The bromine water is decolorized — test for C=C.



Question 22

✅ ANSWER: A

📝 EXPLANATION:  The Cl is on carbon 1 of a 3-carbon chain. IUPAC: 1-chloropropane (number from end giving Cl the lowest number).



Question 23

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Buffer solutions contain a weak acid (HA) and its conjugate base (A⁻). The acid neutralizes added base and the conjugate base neutralizes added acid.



Question 24

✅ ANSWER: A

📝 EXPLANATION:  Glucose is C₆H₁₂O₆ (a hexose monosaccharide). Sucrose is C₁₂H₂₂O₁₁; ribose is C₅H₁₀O₅.



Question 25

✅ ANSWER: D

📝 EXPLANATION:  Boiling point elevation depends on molality of solute particles. 0.2M NaCl gives 0.4 mol/L of ions (NaCl → Na⁺ + Cl⁻), the highest particle concentration.



Question 26

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  CO removes oxygen from Fe₂O₃ (reduces it), while CO itself is oxidized to CO₂. So CO is the reducing agent.



Question 27

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Relative atomic mass is the weighted average mass of an atom of an element compared to 1/12 the mass of carbon-12 (¹²C), by international convention.



Question 28

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Saponification: fat/oil + NaOH (alkali) → soap (sodium fatty acid salt) + glycerol. The term comes from Latin 'sapo' (soap).



Question 29

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Metals are generally malleable (can be beaten into sheets), ductile (drawn into wires), and good conductors. Brittleness is characteristic of ceramics and non-metals, not metals.



Question 30

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  The OH group is on carbon 2 of a 3-carbon chain. IUPAC: propan-2-ol (also called isopropanol or rubbing alcohol).



Question 31

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Calcium (Ca²⁺) produces a brick-red/orange-red flame. K⁺ gives lilac/violet; Na⁺ gives golden yellow; Ba²⁺ gives apple green.



Question 32

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Elements in the same group have the same number of valence electrons, giving them similar chemical properties. E.g., all Group 1 elements have 1 outer electron.



Question 33

✅ ANSWER: A

📝 EXPLANATION:  Halogen reactivity decreases down the group. F is the most reactive (strongest oxidizing agent). Order: F > Cl > Br > I. Fluorine can displace all others from their salts.



Question 34

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Zn + H₂SO₄ → ZnSO₄ + H₂. Zinc displaces hydrogen from dilute acids. The gas produced is hydrogen, confirmed by a squeaky pop with a lit splint.



Question 35

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Fermentation: C₆H₁₂O₆ → 2C₂H₅OH + 2CO₂ (with zymase enzyme, anaerobic). Ethanol and CO₂ are produced. This is the basis of brewing.



Question 36

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Ethylene glycol (ethane-1,2-diol, HOCH₂CH₂OH) is the most common antifreeze. It lowers the freezing point of water significantly.



Question 37

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Percentage yield = (actual yield / theoretical yield) × 100%. Theoretical yield assumes complete reaction; actual yield accounts for losses in real experiments.



Question 38

✅ ANSWER: A

📝 EXPLANATION:  Cracking breaks large alkane molecules (from heavy fractions) into smaller, more useful alkenes and alkanes using heat and catalysts (catalytic cracking).



Question 39

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Both compounds have molecular formula C₂H₆O but different functional groups (-OH in ethanol vs -O- in dimethyl ether). This is functional group isomerism.



Question 40

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Chlorine dissolves in water to form HCl and HOCl (hypochlorous acid), both of which are acidic, turning moist blue litmus paper red.



Question 41

✅ ANSWER: D

📝 EXPLANATION:  During burning, the wick is consumed (gets shorter, not longer). Wax melts (physical change), and combustion of hydrocarbons produces CO₂ and H₂O.



Question 42

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Moles of H₂ = 500/22400 = 0.0223 mol. RMM = mass/moles = 2/0.0223 ≈ 89.7... Wait, RMM of H₂ = 2. Check: moles = 2/2 = 1mol occupies 22400cm³; 500cm³ would be 0.0223mol = 0.0446g. The question tests knowledge: H₂ RMM = 2.



Question 43

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Neutrons = mass number - atomic number = 35 - 17 = 18 neutrons.



Question 44

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Catalysts work by providing an alternative reaction pathway with lower activation energy, allowing more molecules to react at a given temperature. They are not consumed.



Question 45

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Standard state is defined as 25°C (298K) and 1 atm (101.325 kPa). Standard enthalpy values are measured under these conditions.



Question 46

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Addition polymerization occurs when monomers with C=C double bonds (alkenes) open their double bonds and link together. E.g., ethene → polyethylene.



Question 47

✅ ANSWER: A

📝 EXPLANATION:  Esterification: CH₃CH₂OH + CH₃COOH ⇌ CH₃COOC₂H₅ + H₂O. Ethyl ethanoate (ethyl acetate) is formed. Concentrated H₂SO₄ acts as catalyst and dehydrating agent.



Question 48

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Water has 2 bonding pairs and 2 lone pairs. The VSEPR model predicts a bent/V-shaped geometry with bond angle ~104.5°, less than tetrahedral due to lone pair repulsion.



Question 49

✅ ANSWER: D

📝 EXPLANATION:  Hydrogen (H₂, Mr = 2) is the lightest gas. Helium (Mr = 4) is the second lightest. Hydrogen is ~14 times lighter than air.



Question 50

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Half-life (t½) is the time for exactly half of the radioactive atoms in a sample to undergo decay. After each half-life, activity is halved.



Question 51

✅ ANSWER: D

📝 EXPLANATION:  Oxygen (O₂) is not a greenhouse gas. Greenhouse gases include CO₂, CH₄, N₂O, H₂O vapour, and ozone — molecules that absorb and re-emit infrared radiation.



Question 52

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Condensation polymerization links monomers containing -OH and -COOH groups (or similar) with the elimination of small molecules (usually H₂O). E.g., nylon, polyester.



Question 53

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Ester formation (esterification) is a condensation reaction: acid + alcohol → ester + water. A water molecule is expelled (condensed out) when the ester bond forms.



Question 54

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Neutrons = mass number - atomic number = 56 - 26 = 30 neutrons.



Question 55

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Across a period, atomic number (nuclear charge, Z) increases while electrons are added to the same shell. Greater nuclear attraction pulls electrons closer, reducing the radius.



Question 56

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Halogens react with alkanes by substitution (specifically free-radical substitution). A H atom is substituted by Cl. UV light initiates the reaction by breaking Cl₂ into radicals.



Question 57

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Standard enthalpy of combustion (ΔH°c) is the enthalpy change when one mole of substance completely burns in excess oxygen under standard conditions (25°C, 1atm).



Question 58

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  In electrochemical cells: OIL RIG — Oxidation Is Loss (at anode), Reduction Is Gain (at cathode). The anode is where the reducing agent loses electrons.



Question 59

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  In NH₄⁺, nitrogen provides both electrons for the bond with H⁺. This is a coordinate bond (also called a dative covalent bond).



Question 60

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Lavoisier's law: matter is neither created nor destroyed in a chemical reaction. The total mass of reactants equals the total mass of products.



Question 61

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Bakelite is a thermosetting polymer — once set by heat, it cannot be re-melted. It has a cross-linked 3D network structure. Thermoplastics (polyethylene, polystyrene) can be re-melted.



Question 62

✅ ANSWER: A

📝 EXPLANATION:  Fluorine has a very small atomic radius and a high effective nuclear charge, giving it an extremely strong pull on shared electrons in a bond — hence the highest electronegativity.



Question 63

✅ ANSWER: A

📝 EXPLANATION:  Ester hydrolysis (saponification with base, or acid-catalyzed): ester + water → carboxylic acid + alcohol. E.g., CH₃COOC₂H₅ + H₂O → CH₃COOH + C₂H₅OH.



Question 64

✅ ANSWER: D

📝 EXPLANATION:  Endothermic reactions absorb heat from the surroundings (ΔH is positive). The products have more energy than the reactants.



Question 65

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Avogadro's number (NA = 6.022 × 10²³) is the number of constituent particles (atoms, molecules, ions) in one mole of any substance.



Question 66

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  IUPAC prefixes: meth-(1), eth-(2), prop-(3), but-(4), pent-(5), hex-(6), hept-(7), oct-(8), non-(9), dec-(10). Hex = 6 carbons.



Question 67

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Bond polarity increases with electronegativity difference. O is more electronegative than N, which is more electronegative than C. So polarity: O-H > N-H > C-H.



Question 68

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Hot concentrated H₂SO₄ reacts with Cu (a metal below hydrogen in reactivity): Cu + 2H₂SO₄ → CuSO₄ + SO₂ + 2H₂O. SO₂ is produced (not H₂).



Question 69

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Polyethylene (polyethene) is formed by addition polymerization of ethene: n(CH₂=CH₂) → (-CH₂CH₂-)n. The repeating unit is -CH₂CH₂-.



Question 70

✅ ANSWER: A

📝 EXPLANATION:  Molar mass of NH₄NO₃ = 14 + 4 + 14 + 48 = 80g/mol. Two N atoms: 2×14 = 28g. %N = 28/80 × 100 = 35%.



Question 71

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Kc is temperature-dependent. For endothermic reactions (ΔH > 0), increasing temperature shifts equilibrium to the right (products), increasing Kc. For exothermic reactions, Kc decreases with temperature.



Question 72

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Markovnikov's rule: H from HX adds to the carbon that already has more hydrogen atoms (more substituted end gets X). This gives the more stable carbocation intermediate.



Question 73

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Dehydration of ethanol (heating with concentrated H₂SO₄ above 170°C): C₂H₅OH → CH₂=CH₂ + H₂O. Ethene is produced. At lower temperatures (~140°C), diethyl ether forms.



Question 74

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Iron(III) = Fe³⁺; sulfate = SO₄²⁻. To balance: 2 Fe³⁺ and 3 SO₄²⁻ gives charge balance (+6 and -6). Formula: Fe₂(SO₄)₃.



Question 75

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Hydrogenation adds H₂ across a double bond: CH₂=CH₂ + H₂ → CH₃CH₃. Uses Ni/Pt/Pd catalyst. Industrial use: converting vegetable oils to solid fats (margarine).



Question 76

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Atomic radius decreases across a period due to increasing nuclear charge. The smallest radius in a period is typically at Group 17 (halogens), just before the noble gas. (Noble gases have slightly larger apparent radius due to van der Waals radius.)



Question 77

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Diprotic acids donate 2 protons: H₂SO₄ → 2H⁺ + SO₄²⁻. HCl and CH₃COOH are monoprotic (1 H⁺); H₃PO₄ is triprotic (3 H⁺).



Question 78

✅ ANSWER: A

📝 EXPLANATION:  Smelting uses heat and a reducing agent (coke/carbon) to extract metal from ore: Fe₂O₃ + 3CO → 2Fe + 3CO₂. The blast furnace produces pig iron.



Question 79

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Benedict's reagent contains copper(II) sulfate complexed with sodium citrate in alkaline solution. The citrate stabilizes Cu²⁺. Reducing sugars reduce Cu²⁺ to Cu₂O (brick-red precipitate).



Question 80

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  NaCl is highly soluble in water (360g/L). BaSO₄, AgCl, and PbSO₄ are all nearly insoluble, forming precipitates in precipitation reactions.



Question 81

✅ ANSWER: A

📝 EXPLANATION:  Cu is an exception: [Ar]3d¹⁰4s¹ rather than [Ar]3d⁹4s². A fully filled d subshell provides extra stability, so one electron promotes from 4s to complete the 3d.



Question 82

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Ethyne: H-C≡C-H. Bonds: H-C (1σ), C≡C (1σ + 2π), C-H (1σ). Total σ bonds = 3. The triple bond contains one σ and two π bonds.



Question 83

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Nitrogen dioxide (NO₂) is a brown/reddish-brown toxic gas with a pungent smell. It is one of the gases responsible for the brown haze in polluted cities.



Question 84

✅ ANSWER: D

📝 EXPLANATION:  Petroleum gases (methane, ethane, propane, butane) have the lowest boiling points and come off first at the top of the fractionating column. Bitumen has the highest boiling point.



Question 85

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  The Contact process: 2SO₂ + O₂ ⇌ 2SO₃. Catalyst: vanadium(V) oxide (V₂O₅) at 450°C. SO₃ is then absorbed in H₂SO₄ to form oleum, then diluted.



Question 86

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Unsaturated organic compounds contain C=C double bonds or C≡C triple bonds. They can undergo addition reactions (e.g., with Br₂). Alkenes and alkynes are unsaturated.



Question 87

✅ ANSWER: D

📝 EXPLANATION:  BF₃ has 3 bonding pairs and no lone pairs around B. VSEPR theory predicts trigonal planar geometry with 120° bond angles.



Question 88

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Acid-catalyzed addition of water to an alkene is hydration: CH₂=CH₂ + H₂O → CH₃CH₂OH. It follows Markovnikov's rule and is the reverse of dehydration.



Question 89

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  CH₃COCH₃ has a C=O group (ketone) between two CH₃ groups. IUPAC: propanone (3 carbons, ketone functional group). Also called acetone.



Question 90

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Empirical formula is the simplest whole-number ratio. C₆H₆: ratio C:H = 6:6 = 1:1. Empirical formula = CH. Molecular formula = (CH)n where n = 6.



Question 91

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  A homologous series differs by -CH₂- between successive members. CH₄, C₂H₆, C₃H₈ (alkanes) each differ by CH₂ and have the same general formula CₙH₂ₙ₊₂.



Question 92

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Aldehydes reduce Tollens' reagent ([Ag(NH₃)₂]⁺) to produce a silver mirror on the test tube wall. Ketones do not react with Tollens' reagent.



Question 93

✅ ANSWER: A

📝 EXPLANATION:  Molar mass of CO = 12 + 16 = 28g/mol. Moles = 28/28 = 1 mol. Molecules = 1 × 6.02 × 10²³ = 6.02 × 10²³.



Question 94

✅ ANSWER: A

📝 EXPLANATION:  With copper electrodes: cathode: Cu²⁺ + 2e⁻ → Cu (copper deposits). Anode: Cu → Cu²⁺ + 2e⁻ (copper dissolves). The CuSO₄ concentration stays constant. This is used in copper electrorefining.



Question 95

✅ ANSWER: D

📝 EXPLANATION:  Ionic compounds have high melting points; are non-conductors in solid state (ions are fixed); are generally insoluble in organic solvents; and conduct electricity when molten or dissolved (ions become mobile).



Question 96

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Alcohols react with sodium: 2C₂H₅OH + 2Na → 2C₂H₅ONa + H₂. The -OH hydrogen is acidic enough to react with reactive metals like Na.



Question 97

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  In K₂Cr₂O₇: K = +1 (×2 = +2); O = -2 (×7 = -14). So +2 + 2Cr - 14 = 0 → 2Cr = +12 → Cr = +6.



Question 98

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Acidic oxides (usually non-metal oxides like CO₂, SO₂, SO₃) react with bases: CO₂ + 2NaOH → Na₂CO₃ + H₂O. They form acids when dissolved in water.



Question 99

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Equivalent weight = molar mass / valence (n-factor). H₂SO₄ donates 2 H⁺ ions (valence = 2). Eq. wt. = 98/2 = 49g/equivalent.



Question 100

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Each carbon in benzene (C₆H₆) forms 3 σ bonds (sp² hybridized) with angles of 120°. The remaining unhybridized p orbitals form the delocalized π electron system.



Question 101

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Hess's law: the total enthalpy change in a reaction is the same regardless of the route, provided the initial and final states are the same. It is a consequence of energy conservation.



Question 102

✅ ANSWER: D

📝 EXPLANATION:  ¹¹C has mass number 11 (5 neutrons), making it the lightest listed isotope of carbon. ¹²C is the most abundant; ¹⁴C is radioactive. ¹¹C has the lowest mass number.



Question 103

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  NH₄Cl sublimes — it converts directly from solid to gas without melting. Upon cooling, the vapours recombine and deposit as solid NH₄Cl. This distinguishes sublimation from decomposition.



Question 104

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Aluminum is extracted from bauxite (Al₂O₃·2H₂O) by the Hall-Héroult process (electrolysis). Hematite = iron ore; galena = lead ore; chalcopyrite = copper ore.



Question 105

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Hydrogen burns in air (it IS combustible) but does NOT support combustion (it is not an oxidizing agent). Oxygen and fluorine support combustion; hydrogen does not.



Question 106

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Enzymatic browning: phenolase enzymes oxidize phenolic compounds in apple cells when exposed to atmospheric oxygen, producing brown melanin-like compounds.



Question 107

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Alkynes contain a C≡C triple bond. General formula: CₙH₂ₙ₋₂. Alkanes: CₙH₂ₙ₊₂; Alkenes: CₙH₂ₙ. Each degree of unsaturation removes 2 H atoms.



Question 108

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Noble gases (Group 18) have 8 electrons in their outermost shell (except He with 2). This complete octet makes them chemically inert.



Question 109

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  The outermost shell has 2 electrons, placing this element in Group 2 (alkaline earth metals). The 4 shells indicate it's in Period 4 (this is calcium, Ca).



Question 110

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Le Chatelier's principle: if a system at equilibrium is subjected to a change, it will shift to counteract the change and re-establish equilibrium. Catalysts speed up the rate but do not shift equilibrium position.



Question 111

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Moles NaOH = 0.025 × 0.1 = 0.0025 mol. H₂SO₄ + 2NaOH → Na₂SO₄ + 2H₂O. Moles H₂SO₄ = 0.0025/2 = 0.00125 mol. Molarity = 0.00125/0.01 = 0.125M.



Question 112

✅ ANSWER: A

📝 EXPLANATION:  Alpha emission: mass number decreases by 4, atomic number by 2. ²³⁸U (Z=92) → ²³⁴Th (Z=90) + ⁴He. The product is thorium-234.



Question 113

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Iodine solution (iodine dissolved in potassium iodide) turns blue-black in the presence of starch. The iodine molecules fit into the helical structure of amylose in starch.



Question 114

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  The OH is on carbon 2 of a 4-carbon chain (numbering from the end that gives the lowest locant to the OH group). IUPAC name: butan-2-ol. (2-butanol is an acceptable synonym.)



Question 115

✅ ANSWER: D

📝 EXPLANATION:  Phenolphthalein is colourless in acid/neutral solutions and turns pink (magenta) in alkaline solutions (pH > 8.2). It is used to indicate the endpoint of acid-base titrations with strong bases.



Question 116

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  In electroplating, the object to be plated is the cathode, the plating metal is the anode, and the electrolyte contains ions of the plating metal. Metal ions are reduced and deposited on the object.



Question 117

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Aliphatic compounds are open-chain organic compounds (not aromatic). They include alkanes, alkenes, alkynes, and their derivatives. Aromatic compounds contain the benzene ring.



Question 118

✅ ANSWER: A

📝 EXPLANATION:  CaCO₃ → CaO + CO₂. Molar mass CaCO₃ = 100g. Moles = 10/100 = 0.1 mol. Moles CO₂ = 0.1 mol. Volume = 0.1 × 22.4 = 2.24L.



Question 119

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Zinc goes from 0 to +2 (loses electrons) — it is oxidized. Copper goes from +2 to 0 (gains electrons) — it is reduced. Zinc is the reducing agent.



Question 120

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Methane (CH₄) has 4 bonding pairs and no lone pairs. VSEPR predicts regular tetrahedral geometry with bond angles of exactly 109.5°.



Question 121

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Nylon (polyamide) is a condensation polymer formed by reaction between diamine and dicarboxylic acid monomers, releasing water. E.g., nylon-6,6 from hexamethylenediamine and adipic acid.



Question 122

✅ ANSWER: A

📝 EXPLANATION:  Wait — isotopes have the SAME atomic number (proton number) but DIFFERENT mass numbers (and different numbers of neutrons). Option A is incorrect as stated. The correct statement is: isotopes have the same number of protons but different numbers of neutrons.



Question 123

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Hydrogen (H) is the first element in Group 1. Although it has some non-metallic properties, its single outer electron places it in Group 1. Lithium is the first true alkali metal.



Question 124

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  In the Ostwald process (making HNO₃): 4NH₃ + 5O₂ → 4NO + 6H₂O, then 2NO + O₂ → 2NO₂. Nitrogen goes from +2 to +4 — this is oxidation.



Question 125

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  NaCl + AgNO₃ → AgCl↓ + NaNO₃. Silver chloride is a white, insoluble precipitate. This reaction is used as a test for chloride ions.



Question 126

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  NaHCO₃ contains Na⁺ and HCO₃⁻ (hydrogen carbonate ion). IUPAC: sodium hydrogen carbonate. Also commonly called sodium bicarbonate or baking soda.



Question 127

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Gaining electrons adds negative charge, forming an anion (negative ion). E.g., Cl + e⁻ → Cl⁻. Losing electrons forms a cation (positive ion).



Question 128

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  The Arrhenius equation: ln k = ln A - Ea/RT. Plotting ln k vs 1/T gives a straight line with slope = -Ea/R, allowing calculation of activation energy (Ea).



Question 129

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Physical properties can be observed without changing the substance's chemical composition. Melting point, colour, density, and solubility are physical. Flammability and reactivity are chemical properties.



Question 130

✅ ANSWER: A

📝 EXPLANATION:  pOH = 14 - pH = 14 - 11 = 3. [OH⁻] = 10^(-pOH) = 10⁻³ mol/L.



Question 131

✅ ANSWER: A

📝 EXPLANATION:  Mole fraction of NaCl = moles NaCl / total moles = 2/(2+8) = 2/10 = 0.2.



Question 132

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Detergent molecules have a hydrophobic (water-repelling) hydrocarbon tail that attaches to grease, and a hydrophilic (water-attracting) head that dissolves in water, forming micelles that remove grease.



Question 133

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Density at STP = molar mass / molar volume = 44/22.4 = 1.96 g/L. (This corresponds to CO₂, which has molar mass 44g/mol.)



Question 134

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Cl₂ + 2NaOH (cold, dilute) → NaCl + NaClO + H₂O. Sodium chloride and sodium hypochlorite (bleach) are formed. Hot concentrated NaOH gives NaClO₃ instead.



Question 135

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Fractional distillation separates crude oil into fractions (petroleum gases, naphtha, kerosene, diesel, lubricating oils, bitumen) based on the different boiling points of the hydrocarbons.



Question 136

✅ ANSWER: D

📝 EXPLANATION:  Fluorine (F) has the highest electronegativity (4.0 on the Pauling scale) of all elements, due to its small size and high nuclear charge relative to its electron count.



Question 137

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Table salt is sodium chloride (NaCl). It is formed by the reaction of sodium with chlorine and is an essential mineral for human physiology.



Question 138

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Double displacement (metathesis): two ionic compounds exchange partners. AgNO₃ + NaCl → AgCl + NaNO₃. Both cations swap their anions. Ag⁺ + Cl⁻ → AgCl precipitate.



Question 139

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Copper(II) means Cu²⁺. Nitrate = NO₃⁻. To balance: Cu²⁺ + 2NO₃⁻ → Cu(NO₃)₂.



Question 140

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  11460 = 2 × 5730, so 2 half-lives have passed. After 1st: 50g. After 2nd: 25g remains.



Question 141

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  One substance breaks down into simpler substances — this is a decomposition reaction. H₂O₂ decomposes into water and oxygen.



Question 142

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  HCl is a strong acid, fully ionized. [H⁺] = 0.01 = 10⁻² mol/L. pH = -log[H⁺] = -log(10⁻²) = 2.



Question 143

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  HCOOH has one carbon. IUPAC: methanoic acid (meth = 1C, -oic acid = carboxylic acid). Common name is formic acid (from ant stings — Latin: formica = ant).



Question 144

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Nucleophiles are electron-rich species that donate electron pairs to electrophiles. Cl⁻ has lone pairs and negative charge — it is a nucleophile. H⁺, BF₃, and AlCl₃ are electrophiles (electron deficient).



Question 145

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Soap is made by saponification of fats/oils with NaOH. The products are glycerol and sodium salts of long-chain fatty acids (e.g., sodium stearate CH₃(CH₂)₁₆COONa).



Question 146

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Maxwell-Boltzmann distribution: at higher temperatures, a greater fraction of molecules have kinetic energy ≥ activation energy (Ea), leading to more successful collisions per unit time.



Question 147

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Aqua regia (royal water) = 3 parts concentrated HCl + 1 part concentrated HNO₃. It dissolves noble metals like gold and platinum.



Question 148

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Sublimation: solid → gas without passing through liquid phase. Examples: dry ice (CO₂), iodine, ammonium chloride, naphthalene.



Question 149

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Nitrogen forms a triple bond (N≡N) with one sigma and two pi bonds. Each N contributes 3 bonding electrons and has 1 lone pair. Bond energy ~944 kJ/mol.



Question 150

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Water donates H⁺ to form Ca(OH)₂ and H₂. In the Brønsted-Lowry sense, water acts as an acid (H⁺ donor). Ca is oxidized; H is reduced.



Question 151

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Boyle's law: P₁V₁ = P₂V₂. 3 × 2 = P₂ × 1. P₂ = 6atm.



Question 152

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  CH₂O: C = 12, H₂ = 2×1 = 2, O = 16. Total = 12 + 2 + 16 = 30 g/mol.



Question 153

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Biuret test: NaOH + CuSO₄ solution turns violet/purple in the presence of peptide bonds (proteins). The violet color is due to Cu²⁺ complexing with the -CO-NH- peptide bonds.



Question 154

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Potassium permanganate: K⁺ + MnO₄⁻ → KMnO₄. Mn is in +7 oxidation state. It is a strong oxidizing agent used in titrations and as a disinfectant.



Question 155

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  2Na + 2H₂O → 2NaOH + H₂↑. Hydrogen gas is produced. The reaction is vigorous and exothermic; the H₂ may catch fire due to the heat generated.



Question 156

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Organic chemistry is the study of carbon-containing compounds and their reactions. Originally limited to compounds from living organisms; now includes all carbon compounds.



Question 157

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  DBE = (2C + 2 - H)/2 = (2×4 + 2 - 6)/2 = (8+2-6)/2 = 4/2 = 2. This compound has 2 degrees of unsaturation (e.g., 2 double bonds or 1 triple bond).



Question 158

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Chromatography separates mixtures using a mobile phase (carrier) and stationary phase. In liquid chromatography, the mobile phase is a liquid solvent that carries components at different rates through the stationary phase.



Question 159

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Ca²⁺ and PO₄³⁻. LCM of 2 and 3 = 6. Need 3 Ca²⁺ (3×2=+6) and 2 PO₄³⁻ (2×3=-6). Formula: Ca₃(PO₄)₂.



Question 160

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Cathode (negative electrode) attracts cations. H⁺ from water is discharged: 2H⁺ + 2e⁻ → H₂. At the anode: 2Cl⁻ → Cl₂ + 2e⁻. NaOH is produced in the cell.



Question 161

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Iron loses an electron (Fe²⁺ → Fe³⁺). Loss of electrons = oxidation. Iron is oxidized from +2 to +3 oxidation state.



Question 162

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Intensive properties do not depend on the amount of substance (temperature, density, melting point, colour). Volume IS an extensive property — it depends on how much substance is present.



Question 163

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  The fourth N-H bond in NH₄⁺ is a coordinate bond. N donates both electrons to H⁺ (which has no electrons). Once formed, all four N-H bonds are equivalent.



Question 164

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Amphoteric substances react with both acids and bases. Examples: water (H₂O), aluminum oxide (Al₂O₃), amino acids. Water: H₂O + HCl → H₃O⁺ + Cl⁻; H₂O + NaOH → NaOH + OH⁻.



Question 165

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  A single covalent bond consists of 2 shared electrons (one bonding pair). A double bond has 4 electrons (2 pairs); a triple bond has 6 electrons (3 pairs).



Question 166

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Components most soluble in (attracted to) the mobile phase travel furthest, giving highest Rf values. Less polar substances are more soluble in non-polar mobile phases and travel further.



Question 167

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Colligative properties depend on the number of solute particles, not their nature: boiling point elevation, freezing point depression, osmotic pressure, and vapour pressure lowering.



Question 168

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  CH₂=CH₂ + Br₂ → CH₂BrCH₂Br. The double bond opens and two Br atoms add across it. This is an electrophilic addition reaction.



Question 169

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  SO₂ = sulfur dioxide (one S and two O atoms). SO₃ is sulfur trioxide. SO₂ is a pungent gas produced by burning sulfur-containing fuels.



Question 170

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Strong oxidation of ethanol (primary alcohol) with acidified KMnO₄ gives ethanoic acid (CH₃COOH). Mild oxidation with acidified K₂Cr₂O₇ gives ethanal (CH₃CHO).



Question 171

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Mass spectrometry ionizes a sample and separates the ions by their mass-to-charge ratio (m/z). This gives the molar mass of the compound and fragmentation patterns for structural analysis.



Question 172

✅ ANSWER: D

📝 EXPLANATION:  Lattice energy increases with higher charge density. NaF has the smallest F⁻ ion, giving the shortest Na⁺-F⁻ distance and strongest electrostatic attraction — highest lattice energy.



Question 173

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  More alkyl groups donate electrons to the positive carbon (hyperconjugation/induction), stabilizing it. Tertiary (3 alkyl groups) > secondary > primary > methyl (least stable).



Question 174

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  CaC₂ + 2H₂O → Ca(OH)₂ + C₂H₂. Ethyne (acetylene) is produced. This reaction is used to generate acetylene for welding torches.



Question 175

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Fehling's solution (Cu²⁺ in alkaline tartrate) is reduced by reducing sugars (e.g., glucose, fructose, maltose) to form a brick-red precipitate of Cu₂O. Non-reducing sugars (sucrose) do not react.



Question 176

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Electron affinity is the energy released when a gaseous atom gains one electron to form a gaseous anion: X(g) + e⁻ → X⁻(g). Most values are negative (exothermic).



Question 177

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  The standard hydrogen electrode (SHE) is the reference electrode with E° = 0.00V by definition. All other standard electrode potentials are measured relative to it.



Question 178

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Freezing (solidification): liquid → solid by removal of heat. It occurs at the melting/freezing point. For water, the freezing point is 0°C at 1 atm.



Question 179

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Strong acid/weak base titrations have an equivalence point in the acidic range (pH < 7). Methyl orange changes colour in the acid range (3.1–4.4) and is the best choice for this titration type.



Question 180

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Bleaching powder is a mixture of calcium hypochlorite and calcium chloride, represented as Ca(OCl)Cl or CaOCl₂. Made by passing Cl₂ over slaked lime.



Question 181

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Transition metals have partially filled d subshells (or form ions with partially filled d subshells). This gives them variable oxidation states, catalytic activity, and coloured compounds.



Question 182

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  C₄H₁₀ (butane) has 2 structural isomers: n-butane (straight chain: CH₃CH₂CH₂CH₃) and isobutane (branched: (CH₃)₃CH or 2-methylpropane).



Question 183

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Iron catalyst in the Haber process provides a surface for N₂ and H₂ to adsorb and react via a pathway with lower activation energy, increasing the rate without shifting the equilibrium.



Question 184

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Alkanes + halogens (UV light) → free radical substitution. Steps: initiation (X₂ → 2X• by UV), propagation (chain reaction), termination (radicals combine). CH₄ + Cl₂ → CH₃Cl + HCl.



Question 185

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  H adds to C-1 (more H), OH adds to C-2. CH₃-CH=CH₂ + H₂O → CH₃-CH(OH)-CH₃ = propan-2-ol.



Question 186

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  By Graham's law, rate ∝ 1/√M. NH₃ has Mr=17, the smallest of the options. √17 is smallest, so 1/√17 is largest — NH₃ diffuses fastest.



Question 187

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Electrophiles are electron-deficient — they accept electron pairs from nucleophiles. Examples: H⁺, NO₂⁺, Br⁺, Cl⁺, carbocations (R⁺), BF₃, AlCl₃.



Question 188

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  SO₃ + H₂O → H₂SO₄ (sulfuric acid). In the Contact process, SO₃ is absorbed in concentrated H₂SO₄ to form oleum (H₂S₂O₇) which is then diluted to give H₂SO₄.



Question 189

✅ ANSWER: D

📝 EXPLANATION:  Muddy water is a heterogeneous mixture — the solid particles (mud) are not uniformly distributed. Saltwater, air, and alloys are homogeneous mixtures with uniform composition throughout.



Question 190

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Without peroxides (ionic/electrophilic conditions): HBr addition follows Markovnikov's rule — H adds to carbon with more H, Br adds to more substituted carbon. Peroxides cause anti-Markovnikov addition.



Question 191

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Activated charcoal adsorbs gas molecules by weak van der Waals forces (physical adsorption/physisorption). No chemical bonds are formed. Used in gas masks, water filters, and deodorants.



Question 192

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Conjugate acid = base + H⁺. NH₃ + H⁺ → NH₄⁺. So NH₄⁺ is the conjugate acid of NH₃. The conjugate base of NH₃ is NH₂⁻.



Question 193

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Amino acids link through peptide bonds (-CO-NH-) in condensation reactions to form polypeptides and proteins. DNA/RNA are polynucleotides; cellulose/starch are polysaccharides.



Question 194

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  AgBr is cream/pale yellow. AgCl is white, AgI is yellow, AgF is soluble. The test with AgNO₃ distinguishes halides: Cl⁻=white, Br⁻=cream, I⁻=yellow precipitate.



Question 195

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Fe²⁺ → Fe³⁺ involves loss of one electron. This is oxidation (OIL: Oxidation Is Loss of electrons). Rusting oxidizes Fe to Fe²⁺ and then Fe³⁺.



Question 196

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  SN2 (bimolecular nucleophilic substitution) is favoured at primary carbons due to low steric hindrance, allowing backside attack by the nucleophile. SN1 is favoured at tertiary carbons.



Question 197

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Fractional crystallization exploits the difference in solubility with temperature. A mixture dissolved in hot solvent; cooling causes the less soluble component to crystallize out first, separating the components.



Question 198

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Sulfite = SO₃²⁻ (sulfur +4). Sulfate = SO₄²⁻ (sulfur +6). Thiosulfate = S₂O₃²⁻. Bisulfite (hydrogen sulfite) = HSO₃⁻.



Question 199

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Industrial polymerization of ethene (HDPE/LDPE): high temperature, high pressure, with a catalyst (Ziegler-Natta or free radical initiators). These conditions open the C=C bonds.



Question 200

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  A buffer needs a weak acid/base and its salt (conjugate pair). NH₃ (weak base) + NH₄Cl (its conjugate acid salt) forms a basic buffer. The pair resists pH changes on addition of acid or base.



Question 201

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Fe₂O₃: each O = -2 (×3 = -6); 2Fe must = +6; Fe = +3. Name: iron(III) oxide. Common name: hematite or rust.



Question 202

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Mercury (Hg) has a density of ~13,600 kg/m³ — by far the densest of these liquids. It is the only metal that is liquid at room temperature (melting point -39°C).



Question 203

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  The rate determining step (RDS) is the slowest step in a multi-step reaction mechanism. The overall reaction rate cannot be faster than its slowest step, so the RDS limits and determines the overall rate.



Question 204

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Molar mass of H₂O = 2(1) + 16 = 18g/mol. Moles = 54/18 = 3 mol.



Question 205

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  In Zn + CuSO₄ → ZnSO₄ + Cu: Zn is oxidized (0→+2) and Cu²⁺ is reduced (+2→0). Electron transfer occurs — this is a redox reaction. The others are precipitation, neutralization, and decomposition.



Question 206

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Mass number (A) = protons (Z) + neutrons (N). It represents the total number of nucleons in the nucleus. E.g., carbon-12 has 6 protons + 6 neutrons = mass number 12.



Question 207

✅ ANSWER: D

📝 EXPLANATION:  NaOH is a strong base — fully ionized in water: NaOH → Na⁺ + OH⁻. NH₃ is a weak base (partially ionized). Ca(OH)₂ and Mg(OH)₂ are sparingly soluble (limited strong base character).



Question 208

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  sp hybridization involves 2 hybrid orbitals oriented 180° apart — linear geometry. Examples: BeCl₂, CO₂, C₂H₂ (ethyne). The unhybridized p orbitals form π bonds.



Question 209

✅ ANSWER: A

📝 EXPLANATION:  Primary alcohols are oxidized to aldehydes: methanol (CH₃OH) → methanal/formaldehyde (HCHO). Further oxidation gives methanoic acid (HCOOH).



Question 210

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Filtration separates an insoluble solid from a liquid using a filter paper or porous medium. No chemical change occurs — it is a physical separation method.



Question 211

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Benzene's 6 delocalized π electrons form a stable aromatic ring. Addition reactions would disrupt this delocalization and reduce stability. Benzene preferentially undergoes electrophilic substitution.



Question 212

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  C₃H₈ has the formula of an alkane (CₙH₂ₙ₊₂, n=3). Propane = CH₃CH₂CH₃. Propene = C₃H₆; propyne = C₃H₄; cyclopropane = C₃H₆.



Question 213

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Contact process: 2SO₂ + O₂ ⇌ 2SO₃. Catalyst: vanadium(V) oxide (V₂O₅) at about 450°C. Lower temperatures give higher yield but very slow rate; 450°C is the commercial compromise.



Question 214

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Covalent compounds (molecular): low melting/boiling points, do not conduct electricity in solution (no ions), often soluble in organic solvents, and do not consist of ions.



Question 215

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Cathode (reduction): 2H⁺ + 2e⁻ → H₂↑. Anode (oxidation): 2H₂O → O₂ + 4H⁺ + 4e⁻. The cathode product is hydrogen gas.



Question 216

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Glucose = C₆H₁₂O₆ (hexose monosaccharide). Sucrose = C₁₂H₂₂O₁₁; ribose = C₅H₁₀O₅; cellulose/starch = (C₆H₁₀O₅)ₙ.



Question 217

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Catenation is carbon's unique ability to form stable bonds with other carbon atoms, creating long chains, branched chains, and rings. This is why millions of organic compounds exist.



Question 218

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Zinc powder has much greater surface area than lumps. Greater surface area = more collisions per second between reactant particles = faster reaction rate.



Question 219

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Electrolysis of CuSO₄: cathode: Cu²⁺ + 2e⁻ → Cu. Electric current drives the non-spontaneous deposition of copper metal from the solution.



Question 220

✅ ANSWER: D

📝 EXPLANATION:  Oxidation states of N: NH₃ = -3, N₂O = +1, NO₂ = +4, HNO₃: H=+1, O=-2 each. H + N + 3(-2) = 0 → N = +5. HNO₃ has the highest oxidation state for N (+5).



Question 221

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  The -NH₂ (amino) group in amino acids is basic — it accepts protons (H⁺). The -COOH (carboxyl) group is acidic. This dual functionality makes amino acids amphoteric (zwitterions at certain pH).



Question 222

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Hot acidified K₂Cr₂O₇ is a strong oxidizing agent that oxidizes primary alcohols all the way to carboxylic acids: R-CH₂OH → RCHO → RCOOH. (Mild oxidation stops at the aldehyde.)



Question 223

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  CaCO₃: Ca = 40, C = 12, O₃ = 3×16 = 48. Total = 40 + 12 + 48 = 100 g/mol.



Question 224

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Exothermic reactions release heat to the surroundings (ΔH is negative). The temperature of the surroundings increases. Products are at a lower energy level than reactants.



Question 225

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Atomic number (Z) = number of protons in the nucleus. In a neutral atom, Z also equals the number of electrons.



Question 226

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Cathode: Na⁺ + e⁻ → Na. Anode: 2Cl⁻ → Cl₂ + 2e⁻. Chlorine gas is produced at the positive electrode (anode).



Question 227

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  The SI unit of amount of substance is the mole (mol). One mole contains 6.022 × 10²³ particles (Avogadro's constant).



Question 228

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Fossil fuels are formed from ancient organic matter: coal, petroleum (crude oil), and natural gas. Hydrogen gas is not a fossil fuel; it can be produced from water by electrolysis.



Question 229

✅ ANSWER: D

📝 EXPLANATION:  P₄O₁₀ can be correctly named phosphorus(V) oxide (each P is +5), phosphorus pentoxide (old common name), or tetraphosphorus decoxide (IUPAC prefix method).



Question 230

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  P₄ + 5O₂ → P₄O₁₀. When phosphorus burns in oxygen, white fumes of phosphorus(V) oxide are produced. Very dense white smoke is observed.



Question 231

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  NaOH is a strong base: [OH⁻] = 0.001 = 10⁻³. pOH = 3. pH = 14 - 3 = 11.



Question 232

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Neutralization: acid + base → salt + water. E.g., HCl + NaOH → NaCl + H₂O. The H⁺ from acid combines with OH⁻ from base to form water.



Question 233

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Moles = mass / molar mass = 27/27 = 1 mol.



Question 234

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Gamma rays (γ) are electromagnetic radiation with the highest penetrating power — they can pass through several centimeters of lead. Alpha particles are stopped by paper; beta by thin aluminum.



Question 235

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Brønsted-Lowry theory: acids are proton (H⁺) donors; bases are proton acceptors. This extends the concept of acids to non-aqueous solutions.



Question 236

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  The law of mass action states that reaction rate is proportional to the product of reactant concentrations raised to appropriate powers. This is the basis of rate equations.



Question 237

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Hydrogen burns with a characteristic squeaky pop when a lighted splint is held to the test tube. This is the standard lab test for identifying hydrogen gas.



Question 238

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  CH₃NH₂ contains the -NH₂ (amino) group attached to an alkyl group (CH₃). This is a primary amine. IUPAC name: methanamine (or methylamine).



Question 239

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Elements are arranged in order of increasing atomic number (number of protons). The period is determined by number of electron shells; the group by number of outer electrons.



Question 240

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Complete combustion: C₃H₈ + 5O₂ → 3CO₂ + 4H₂O. All carbon becomes CO₂ and all hydrogen becomes H₂O. Balance: C: 3=3✓; H: 8=8✓; O: 10=10✓.



Question 241

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Oxidation (classical definition) = addition of oxygen or removal of hydrogen. E.g., C + O₂ → CO₂ (carbon oxidized). Modern definition: loss of electrons.



Question 242

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Litmus is purple/violet in neutral solutions. It turns red in acidic solutions (pH < 7) and blue in alkaline solutions (pH > 7).



Question 243

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  In graphite, each carbon forms 3 covalent bonds (sp² hybrid), leaving one delocalized electron per carbon in a π system. These mobile electrons allow graphite to conduct electricity.



Question 244

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  The Ostwald process converts ammonia to nitric acid: 4NH₃ + 5O₂ → 4NO + 6H₂O (Pt/Rh catalyst); 2NO + O₂ → 2NO₂; 3NO₂ + H₂O → 2HNO₃ + NO.



Question 245

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  In galvanic (voltaic) cells: anode = oxidation (negative terminal); cathode = reduction (positive terminal). This is opposite to electrolytic cells where external current is applied.



Question 246

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Ethanol = C₂H₅OH or CH₃CH₂OH. 'Eth-' prefix indicates 2 carbon atoms.



Question 247

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Electrolytes conduct electricity when dissolved in water or when molten, because ions become free to move. Strong electrolytes fully dissociate; weak electrolytes partially dissociate.



Question 248

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Alcohols (alkanols) have the general formula CₙH₂ₙ₊₁OH (or CₙH₂ₙ₊₂O), with a hydroxyl (-OH) group. E.g., methanol CH₃OH (n=1), ethanol C₂H₅OH (n=2).



Question 249

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  The SI unit of pressure is the Pascal (Pa). 1 Pa = 1 N/m². Standard atmosphere = 101,325 Pa ≈ 1.01 × 10⁵ Pa.



Question 250

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Molecular formula shows the exact number of each type of atom in a molecule. E.g., glucose: molecular = C₆H₁₂O₆; empirical = CH₂O. The molecular formula is a whole-number multiple of the empirical formula.



Question 251

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Homogeneous catalysis: catalyst and reactants are in the same phase (usually all in solution or all gas). Heterogeneous catalysis: catalyst is in a different phase (e.g., solid catalyst with gaseous reactants).



Question 252

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Transition metals have variable oxidation states (e.g., Fe: +2 and +3; Mn: +2, +3, +4, +6, +7). Having only ONE oxidation state is NOT a characteristic — that applies to Group 1/2 metals.



Question 253

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Dialysis uses a semi-permeable membrane that allows small molecules/ions to pass through but retains larger molecules (e.g., proteins). Used in kidney dialysis machines.



Question 254

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Ca has 20 electrons. Ca²⁺ has lost 2 electrons, leaving 20 - 2 = 18 electrons.



Question 255

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  AgI is a yellow precipitate (insoluble). AgCl = white; AgBr = cream/pale yellow; AgI = yellow. The increasing colour intensity down the halide group is a useful memory aid.



Question 256

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Limewater (Ca(OH)₂ solution) turns milky (white precipitate of CaCO₃) when CO₂ is bubbled through it. This is the standard test for carbon dioxide.



Question 257

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Stainless steel is an alloy of iron (main), chromium (~11-18%), and often nickel. The chromium forms a protective Cr₂O₃ layer, preventing rusting.



Question 258

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Sucrose (table sugar) = C₁₂H₂₂O₁₁. It is a disaccharide formed from glucose + fructose with elimination of H₂O.



Question 259

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  The downward arrow (↓) after a formula in a chemical equation indicates that the substance is a precipitate — an insoluble solid that forms in solution.



Question 260

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Noble gases occupy Group 0 (old notation) or Group 18 (IUPAC notation) of the periodic table. They have complete outer electron shells, making them chemically inert.



Question 261

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  HNO₃ is nitric acid. HNO₂ is nitrous acid. Nitric acid is the stronger acid and has N in +5 oxidation state; nitrous acid has N in +3 state.



Question 262

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  One molecule of CO₂ has 3 atoms (1C + 2O). One mole of CO₂ has 6.02 × 10²³ molecules × 3 atoms/molecule = 3 × 6.02 × 10²³ atoms = 1.806 × 10²⁴ atoms.



Question 263

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Cellulose is a natural polymer of glucose, found in plant cell walls. Nylon, polythene, and PVC are synthetic (man-made) polymers.



Question 264

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Cu + 8HNO₃(dilute) → 3Cu(NO₃)₂ + 2NO + 4H₂O. Dilute HNO₃ gives NO (colorless); concentrated HNO₃ gives NO₂ (brown).



Question 265

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  K₂SO₄ = potassium sulfate. K is +1 (no Roman numeral needed for fixed oxidation state metals). SO₄²⁻ = sulfate. Name: potassium sulfate.



Question 266

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Complete combustion of methane: CH₄ + 2O₂ → CO₂ + 2H₂O. Balance: C 1=1✓; H 4=4✓; O 4=4✓. This reaction releases heat and is the basis of natural gas as a fuel.



Question 267

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Neutrons are subatomic particles found in the nucleus with no electrical charge (neutral). Protons have +1 charge; electrons have -1 charge; ions carry net charge.



Question 268

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Thermite reaction: 2Al + Fe₂O₃ → Al₂O₃ + 2Fe + heat. Aluminum reduces iron oxide. The reaction releases enormous heat and molten iron — used for welding railway tracks.



Question 269

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Concentrated H₂SO₄ is a powerful dehydrating agent. It removes hydrogen and oxygen as water from sugar (C₁₂H₂₂O₁₁ → 12C + 11H₂O), leaving a black porous carbon column.



Question 270

✅ ANSWER: D

📝 EXPLANATION:  Bromothymol blue changes from yellow (acidic, pH < 6) to blue (alkaline, pH > 7.6). It is used for titrations near neutral pH. Litmus is red in acid, blue in alkali.



Question 271

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Iodine solution (iodine in KI) turns blue-black in the presence of starch. The triiodide ions (I₃⁻) fit into the helical structure of starch's amylose fraction, causing the colour.



Question 272

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Bromine (Br₂) is the only non-metal that is liquid at room temperature (melting point -7°C, boiling point 59°C). It is a red-brown, dense, volatile liquid.



Question 273

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Molar mass H₂O = 18g/mol. Mass of O = 16. %O = 16/18 × 100 = 88.9%.



Question 274

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Fluorine (F₂) and chlorine (Cl₂) are gases at room temperature. Bromine is liquid; iodine is solid. Chlorine is the most commonly tested gaseous Group 17 element.



Question 275

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Alkynes have the general formula CₙH₂ₙ₋₂. Simplest: n=2, giving C₂H₂ = ethyne (acetylene), H-C≡C-H. There is no 'methyne' as carbon cannot form a triple bond with only 1 carbon.



Question 276

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Avogadro's law: equal volumes of all gases under the same conditions (T and P) contain the same number of molecules. This is why 1 mole of any gas at STP occupies 22.4L.



Question 277

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Fe²⁺ (iron(II)) ions give a pale green colour in solution. Fe³⁺ (iron(III)) ions give a yellow/brown colour. This colour difference is useful in identifying oxidation states of iron.



Question 278

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Photosynthesis: 6CO₂ + 6H₂O + light energy → C₆H₁₂O₆ + 6O₂. Plants absorb CO₂ and water, using light energy to produce glucose and release oxygen.



Question 279

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Concentrated HNO₃ is colourless when freshly prepared but develops a yellow/brown tint due to slight decomposition: 4HNO₃ → 4NO₂ + 2H₂O + O₂. The NO₂ dissolves giving the yellow colour.



Question 280

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Catalytic converters (Pt/Pd/Rh catalysts) convert toxic exhaust gases: 2CO + 2NO → 2CO₂ + N₂. Carbon monoxide and nitrogen oxides are converted to harmless CO₂ and N₂.



Question 281

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Na has 11 electrons: 2 in shell 1, 8 in shell 2, 1 in shell 3. Electronic configuration: 2,8,1. The single outer electron explains sodium's +1 oxidation state and reactivity.



Question 282

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  The double arrow ⇌ indicates a reversible reaction (equilibrium). The Haber process N₂ + 3H₂ ⇌ 2NH₃ is reversible — both forward and backward reactions occur. The others proceed to completion.



Question 283

✅ ANSWER: A

📝 EXPLANATION:  Hard water Ca²⁺/Mg²⁺ ions + soap (R-COO⁻Na⁺) → R-COO⁻)₂Ca²⁺ (insoluble scum). This wastes soap and leaves grey deposits. Synthetic detergents form soluble Ca/Mg salts.



Question 284

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  CH₃COOH: 2C = 24, 4H = 4, 2O = 32. Total = 24 + 4 + 32 = 60 g/mol.



Question 285

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  By definition, organic compounds are carbon-containing compounds. Carbon is the backbone of all organic molecules, forming chains, rings, and complex structures.



Question 286

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Vegetable oils contain unsaturated C=C bonds. Catalytic hydrogenation (H₂ + Ni catalyst) adds hydrogen across C=C bonds, converting liquid unsaturated oils to solid/semi-solid saturated fats.



Question 287

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Charles' law: at constant pressure, volume is directly proportional to temperature (in Kelvin). V₁/T₁ = V₂/T₂. Heating increases average kinetic energy, causing the gas to expand.



Question 288

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Atomic radius decreases across a period. Sodium (Group 1) has the smallest nuclear charge relative to its outer electrons in Period 3, giving it the largest atomic radius.



Question 289

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Water's concentration is constant (55.5 mol/L, essentially), so it is incorporated into Kw. The ionic product of water: Kw = [H⁺][OH⁻] = 10⁻¹⁴ at 25°C.



Question 290

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Activation energy (Ea) is the minimum energy that colliding molecules must possess for a successful reaction to occur. Catalysts lower Ea, increasing reaction rate.



Question 291

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Galvanizing coats iron with zinc. Zinc oxidizes in preference to iron (it is higher in the reactivity series), providing cathodic/sacrificial protection even when the coating is scratched.



Question 292

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  CH₄: carbon forms 4 bonds with 4 H atoms, using all 4 valence electrons for bonding. No lone pairs on carbon. H₂O has 2 lone pairs; NH₃ has 1; PCl₅ has 0 but is expanded octet.



Question 293

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Sucrose (disaccharide) + H₂O (invertase enzyme or acid) → glucose + fructose. The mixture of equal parts glucose and fructose is called invert sugar.



Question 294

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  The net ionic equation eliminates spectator ions (Na⁺ and NO₃⁻). Only Ag⁺ and Cl⁻ actually react to form the precipitate: Ag⁺(aq) + Cl⁻(aq) → AgCl(s).



Question 295

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Ammonia (NH₃) is an alkaline gas. It turns moist red litmus paper blue. CO₂ and SO₂ turn litmus red (acidic gases); Cl₂ bleaches it.



Question 296

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Standard electrode potentials are measured at 25°C (298K), 1 atm pressure, and 1.0 M concentration of relevant ions (or unit activity).



Question 297

✅ ANSWER: D

📝 EXPLANATION:  Ethene: H₂C=CH₂. Each C-H bond = 1σ (4 total). The C=C bond contains 1σ + 1π. Total sigma bonds = 4(C-H) + 1(C=C) = 5σ bonds.



Question 298

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  NH₄Cl is an ionic compound. NH₄⁺ (ammonium ion) and Cl⁻ (chloride ion) are held together by ionic bonding (electrostatic attraction between oppositely charged ions).



Question 299

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Washing soda is Na₂CO₃·10H₂O (hydrated). On heating, it loses all water to give anhydrous sodium carbonate (Na₂CO₃), which is the anhydrous form.



Question 300

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Empirical formula mass of CH₂ = 12+2 = 14. n = molecular mass/empirical mass = 56/14 = 4. Molecular formula = (CH₂)₄ = C₄H₈.



Question 301

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  2CH₃COOH + Na₂CO₃ → 2CH₃COONa + H₂O + CO₂. Acid + carbonate → salt + water + CO₂. Sodium ethanoate (sodium acetate) is the salt produced.



Question 302

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  The nucleus contains protons (+) and neutrons (neutral). The nuclear charge = number of protons × (+1 charge) = atomic number Z.



Question 303

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  EMF = Electromotive Force. It is the potential difference (voltage) of an electrochemical cell under no-load conditions, measured in volts (V). It drives the flow of electrons in the external circuit.



Question 304

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Dinitrogen = 2N; tetroxide = 4O. Formula: N₂O₄. It exists in equilibrium with NO₂: N₂O₄ ⇌ 2NO₂ (colorless ⇌ brown gas).



Question 305

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Electronegativity increases across a period (more nuclear charge, same shell) and decreases down a group (more shielding). F(4.0) > O(3.5) > N(3.0).



Question 306

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  First stage of Ostwald process: 4NH₃ + 5O₂ → 4NO + 6H₂O. Catalyst: platinum-rhodium (Pt/Rh) gauze at 850-900°C. The catalyst oxidizes ammonia to nitrogen monoxide.



Question 307

✅ ANSWER: B

📝 EXPLANATION:  Decomposition (analysis) reaction: one compound breaks into two or more simpler substances. E.g., 2H₂O₂ → 2H₂O + O₂; CaCO₃ → CaO + CO₂.



Question 308

✅ ANSWER: D

📝 EXPLANATION:  PCl₅ has 5 bond pairs and no lone pairs on P. P uses sp³d hybridization (expands octet using d orbitals). Geometry: trigonal bipyramidal with bond angles 90° and 120°.



Question 309

✅ ANSWER: C

📝 EXPLANATION:  Hydrolysis of starch: (C₆H₁₀O₅)ₙ + nH₂O → nC₆H₁₂O₆. Amylase enzymes catalyze the hydrolysis of glycosidic bonds in starch, producing glucose units.
